Let's design a binary support search routine:
- n maybe 1024 or 2048. Support size m chosen. Equal weights a_i=1/m for i∈S.
- Maintain count array R = convolution of S,S (integer counts) length 2n-1. Rmax.
- Score = 2n Rmax / m^2.
- Local search: For current active sums k where R_k ≥ Rmax - delta, find candidate flips.
   - Add a position p not in S: It increases R_{p+q} by 2 for each q∈S (and R_{2p} by 1) if q? For binary convolution, adding bit p adds 2*S shifted at p plus S[p]^2? Specifically new R = old + conv(e_p, S) + conv(S, e_p) + e_p*e_p = old + 2*S shifted plus 1 at 2p (if p not in S). If p in S removal similar.
   - We can evaluate a flip in O(popcount) or O(n) using precomputed? O(m) per flip. For n=1024,m~500, too slow for many. But can use FFT to compute new R O(n log n) per flip, maybe 0.1ms? Too slow for thousands. Instead local: choose random p, update active counts; accept if Rmax decreases or active sum decreases. We can maintain R efficiently: when flipping p, update all counts R[p+q] for q∈S (O(m)) and if add O(m), remove O(m). m~n/2, O(1000) per flip. 1800s can do ~1e6 flips (1e9 ops too high in Python). Need vectorized updates: Use numpy array S indices; R[p+indices] += 2 (but indices may overflow length; need mask). Vectorized O(m) in C, 1000 operations; 1e5 flips ~0.1s? Actually numpy overhead per flip ~10us? Could do 100k. Good.
   - But R has length 2n; p+idx might exceed. Use idx = S; valid = idx <= 2n-2-p; R[p+idx[valid]] += 2. Also if not in S, R[2p] +=1. For remove subtract.
   - Rmax update can be recompute max each flip O(2n) in C, okay 1000. 100k flips*1000=1e8 C ops okay maybe seconds.
   - Simulated annealing: try random flips (add/remove) and accept if score improves or with probability. Track best Rmax and support.
   - To avoid O(m) memory each flip, use S boolean and indices.

Implementation of local optimizer:
```
def optimize_local(a, time_limit, rng, state, use_flicker=True):
    a = normalize(a)
    n = a.size
    c = conv(a,a); M=max; best...
    momentum = zeros(n)
    step = 0.05
    temp = 0.1*M
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
         c = conv(a,a); M=max
         # if improve, reduce step/temp
         active = c >= M - 0.02*M maybe top 20
         # weights: softmax((c-M)/temp)
         p = exp((c-M)/temp); p/=sum
         grad = conv_grad(p,a)  # returns length n: sum_j p_{i+j} a_j *2? We'll compute without 2, include in step.
         # center grad to keep sum? Since normalization, constant component irrelevant. Use grad -= mean(grad)
         # momentum = beta*mom + (1-beta)*grad
         # Try multiplicative and subtractive steps:
         for scale in [0.1,0.3,0.7,1.0]:
             h = a * exp(-scale*step*mom) ; normalize; if score better update
             h = a - scale*step*mom; clip; normalize; if score better
         # if no improvement for many, random perturb/flicker support
```
Need efficient gradient via FFT:
```
def conv_gradient_weights(p, a):
    # p length 2n-1 weights for sums k, a length n
    # want g_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} p_{i+j} a_j
    n=a.size; m=p.size
    size = next_pow2(n+m-1)
    # Use convolution of a with p_rev: r = a * p_rev
    # r[t]=sum_j a_j p_rev[t-j]
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    r = ifft0(fft(a)*fft(p_rev))
    # g_i = r[m-1 - i]? Earlier p_rev = p[2n-2 - t]. Let's test small n=2, a=[a0,a1], p=[p0,p1,p2]
    # g0 = p0 a0 + p1 a1? For i=0: j=0 p0 a0, j=1 p1 a1. yes.
    # g1 = p1 a0 + p2 a1.
    # p_rev=[p2,p1,p0]. conv a*p_rev length4:
    # t0=a0p2
    # t1=a0p1 + a1p2 =? g? m-1=2. r[?]. r[2]=a0p0 + a1p1 =g0? r[1]=a0p1+a1p2 =? for n=2, g? r[1] = a0p1+a1p2, not g1 (a? g1=a0p1? wait p_{1+j}: j=0 p1 a0, j=1 p2 a1 yes r[1]. r[2-1=1]=g1. r[1]=g1. r[? m-1-i =1 for i=1 yes. For i=0, r[2]=a0p0+a1p1 =? g0 = a0p0 + a1p1 yes. So g_i = r[m-1-i]. Good.
    return r[m-1 - np.arange(n)]
```
Then gradient = 2*g. We can ignore factor.

Convolution a*a via FFT: ensure real:
```
def convolve(a):
   n=a.size
   size=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
   return c[:2*n-1]
```
For many calls, choose size = next power of two ≥2n-1. For n=4096 size=8192.

Scoring: internal F = n * max(c) (sum=1). Official =2nM. We can compare.

Let's design binary support local search in detail, as it may yield dramatic improvement. We need find S with low max coefficient. We can use **greedy repair**:
- Start from random support size m.
- Compute R and Rmax.
- While time: identify top active sums. For each active sum k, the pairs (i,j) with i+j=k and both in S contribute. To reduce R_k, we can remove one of the elements from the support or add elements? Removal reduces m too, changing score. Better keep m fixed: swap an element p out with q in. A swap changes counts by -removal + addition. We can evaluate swap candidates.
- For high sum k, any p in S such that k-p in S is on a pair; removing p will reduce R_k by 2? plus effects at 2p and p+others. Adding q not in S will increase sums q+others. We can choose q that adds to low sums.
- Simplify: random p in S, random q not in S, accept if new Rmax≤old. Vectorized evaluation of swap can be O(m) using temporary copies? To avoid copying full R, we can compute change contributions from p removal and q addition; but max after changes requires updating. We can update R in place for p removal, compute max, then apply q addition, compute max, if worse revert. That's O(m) each + two max scans. Might be okay for n=1024 and few thousand swaps.
- Use annealing to escape.

But maybe simpler: ** minimize max count via max-flow?** For each target threshold T, can we add/remove to ensure all sums ≤T? This is hitting set: for each sum >T, remove at least one from its pairs. Could greedily remove elements involved in most overfull sums. This is like set cover. We can start with full support? Remove to reduce peaks. But support size too small increases score. We can choose m and remove until Rmax ≤ threshold. Use greedy: While some R_k > T, pick an index i whose removal reduces the largest violation most (sum over k of max(R_k-T,0) involving i), remove it (or move). This constructs sparse sets with flat sum counts. Then maybe add back to increase m to optimal score. This is promising!

Piecewise optimizer details:

Binary support greedy removal/addition:
```
def support_counts_from_boolean(Sbool, n):
    a = Sbool.astype(float)
    c = convolve(a)
    return c

def greedy_support(n, target_ratio=0.75, m_init=None, max_remove=?, max_iter=?, rng=None, time_limit=...):
    # start with boolean array of size n, maybe random with prob p=0.5 or all ones.
    # compute c float but counts integer.
    # target max = target_ratio * m*m / n (float)
    # While some c > target and iter:
    #   d = max(0, c - target) (only violations)
    #   grad = conv_gradient_weights(d, a_bool)  # sum_q d_{i+q} a_q
    #   V = 2*grad - d[2*i] (where 2i<len)
    #   choose i with a_i=1 and max V
    #   if max V <= 0: break
    #   set a_i=0, m-=1, update c manually: subtract contributions
    #   recompute target with new m (because score target changes)
    # After removal, optionally add positions to increase m while maintaining c<=target*margin.
```
But the target depends on m: to achieve score≤S, need Rmax ≤ S*m^2/(2n). For greedy, we might target a specific score e.g. 1.45. As m decreases, target changes non-monotonically (m^2). So maybe keep m fixed by swaps rather than removal.

Greedy **swap** with fixed m: When a violation exists, remove the most contributing 1 bit and add the best 0 bit to keep m. This is complex but doable.
Alternative: start with m desired, random support; while c>target, perform swaps: choose a 1 to remove with high V (reduces violations), and a 0 to add with low increase. - Maintain S of size m. For each iteration, compute c. We can implement with random sampling if full search O(n^2) too slow.
- Cost to remove i: reduction vector Δrem_i(k) = 2*contribution_i(k) - self. New max maybe. Cost to add q: increase vector Δadd_q(k)=2*contribution_q(k)+self. For fixed m, swap effect = Δadd - Δrem. Let's detail piecewise optimizer to be effective. Use n=256 or 512 for optimization (score computed exactly discrete). The discrete score for piecewise with segments lengths/heights can be evaluated fast. Nelder-Mead may get stuck; use multiple random initial points and also random coordinate search.

Parameterization: K segments cover n bins. Boundaries: use K-1 logit values; cumulative fractions via sigmoid, sorted automatically if cumulative? If logit independent, sorting needed. Better parameterize segment lengths with softmax of K values to guarantee positive sum. Heights with softmax of K*alpha (or exp logit) times scale. Then normalize sequence. Sequence is piecewise constant with lengths `l_i = max(1, round(softmax(length_logits)*n))` adjust sum. Heights `h_i = exp(height_logits)`; normalize whole vector.
This is smooth-ish but softmax rounding. Could instead use continuous lengths floating and assign bins by floor; objective non-smooth. Nelder-Mead okay.
To allow zero segments, clamp lengths after rounding to 0 if small; but softmax positive. We can let some segments zero by threshold if length <1.

We can also optimize directly with log-lengths positive and log-heights positive:
`lengths = exp(log_len) / sum(exp(log_len)) * n`, round; `heights = exp(log_h)`. Normalize.
Nelder-Mead on log params. Random initial log_len uniform 0, log_h uniform -1..1.

But for monotone 3-block, we need some widths small; log can.

We might not need scipy; implement a simple coordinate/hill climb for piecewise: random param, then perturb parameters one by one and accept if score improves, with simulated annealing. Dimension small, many evals. This avoids scipy dependency and closures. But scipy available. Use both? Simpler to use `scipy.optimize.minimize` top-level. Need pass n,K as args. Objective function:
```
def piecewise_objective(params, n, K):
    log_len = params[:K]
    log_h = params[K:2*K]
    l = np.exp(log_len - max)
    l_obs = l / l.sum() * n
    l_int = np.maximum(1, np.round(l_obs).astype(int))
    # adjust to sum n
    ...
    h = np.exp(log_h)
    seq = np.repeat(h, l_int)  # if sum l_int maybe n; adjust last
    seq = seq[:n]
    seq = normalize_nonneg(seq)
    return compute_score(seq)
```
`np.repeat` with int lengths. If some lengths 1, okay. To allow zero, maybe not.
Rounding could make objective noisy; Nelder-Mead may still.
We can add a **continuous piecewise exact score** for optimization to reduce noise: For piecewise constant on continuous interval, compute g(t) analytically? Could sample fine t and max. But discrete n=512 is fine.

We can run `scipy.optimize.minimize` with method 'Powell' or 'Nelder-Mead'. Powell maybe better for non-smooth. Options. Each run 100-200 evals. Try K=3,4,5,8 for n=256/512. Use best.

But piecewise covering all n with no zeros might not capture sparse support. We can add a **gap support piecewise**: K blocks separated by zeros. Parameterize blocks with lengths and gaps. Could be too many.

Alternatively, treat zero segments as very low heights. Full optimize will set some near zero naturally.

Pseudo code for propose_candidate:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    hard_deadline = time.monotonic()+budget_s-60
    state=...
    # initial provided
    provided=load...
    # baseline sequences
    add baseline [1/3,2/3], ones(8), etc
    # piecewise optimization quick
    for n in (256,512):
       for K in (3,4,5):
           for restart in range(5):
              params random
              res=minimize(piecewise_objective, params, args=(n,K), method='Powell', options={'maxiter':80, 'ftol':1e-8})
              seq=piecewise_seq(res.x,n,K); update state
              if time remaining
    # local refine candidates at different n
    candidate_list = [provided, piecewise_best, baseline, random starts]
    for n in (512,1024,2048):
       seq=resize best or start; optimize_local(seq, limit, rng, state)
    # binary support search maybe if time
    for n in (1024,2048):
       support = binary_anneal(n, time_limit, rng, state)
       if support is not None:
          a = support float normalize; update state; optimize_local(a, ...)
    # final upsample best progressively up to 8192 with local
    while time:
       cur_n=state.seq.size
       next_n=min(8192, max(1024, 2*cur_n))
       seq=resize_to_length(state.seq,next_n)
       optimize_local(seq, time_limit=..., rng, state)
       if no gain break
    return state.seq as list floats
```
Need be careful not to spend all time on scipy minimize; set small budget.

Local optimizer implementation detailed:
```
def optimize_local(a, time_limit, rng, state, max_iters=None):
    a = normalize_nonneg(np.asarray(a,float))
    n=a.size
    if n<2: return
    c=conv_fft(a); best=...
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    it=0; no_imp=0; temp=0.15
    momentum=np.zeros(n)
    beta=0.8
    step=0.1
    # maybe random_target = current_score * 0.995? no
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        # compute c if not already? Use c from previous after update
        M=c.max()
        cur= n*M
        if cur < best - 1e-12: update state; no_imp=0; step=max(0.02,step*0.98); temp=max(0.02,temp*0.95)
        else: no_imp +=1
        if no_imp>40: # perturb
             a = random_perturb(a,rng,0.15) or support flicker
             c=conv_fft(a); step=...; continue
        # active weights
        # choose temp absolute relative to M: tau = max(M*0.02, 1e-12/temp?)
        tau = max(1e-12, M * 0.05 + 1e-14*temp)
        e=(c-M)/tau
        e=np.clip(e,-30,0)
        p=np.exp(e); p/=p.sum()
        # gradient
        g=2*conv_grad(p,a)
        g -= np.mean(g)  # constant irrelevant
        momentum = beta*momentum + (1-beta)*g
        scale_norm = np.max(np.abs(momentum))
        if scale_norm<1e-14: perturb
        # try candidate updates
        for s in (0.02,0.08,0.2,0.5):
             # multiplicative
             logf = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-15)) - s*scale_norm*momentum
             logf -= np.max(logf)
             h=np.exp(logf); h/=h.sum()
             sc=score normalized (n*maxconv)
             if <best_in_trail: candidate
             # subtractive
             h=a - s*scale_norm*momentum; h=np.clip(h,0,None); h/=h.sum()
             sc...
        if found: a=best_candidate; c=conv_fft(a); no_imp=0
        else:
             # small random
             a=random_perturb(a,rng,0.05); c=...
    final update
```
This uses multiple FFTs per iteration (score candidates). Could be okay. We can reduce by only trying multiplicative (often good) and a subtractive when no improve.
Need score function cached? Not.

Multiplicative update with gradient centered: `log(a) - s*momentum`. Since gradient large where a should decrease. If momentum positive high, log decreases. Good.
Subtractive may create negatives; clip.
Line search: choose best among s values; if none better than current, try smaller s or random.
We can keep `trail_score` current (n*M) and update if any candidate < trail_score.

Active weights: p over all c with softmax; for large n, many small; okay. We can zero out p where c < M - 0.25*M to reduce noise:
```
p[c < M - 0.25*M]=0
if p.sum()==0: p = uniform top1? 
```
Use temp.

Random perturb functions:
- Gaussian multiplicative: `a *= exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, n)); normalize`.
- Block perturb: choose random segment multiply by exp(sigma*normal).
- Support flicker: set a[random indices] *= factor (0 or large) to explore zeros. For nonnegative, setting zeros can help. Use with care.

We can also implement **coordinate dead/utility perturb**:
```
def support_flicker(a,rng,rate=0.05):
    idx = rng.random(n)<rate

Local search: For each step, pick an active index i? Let's use random swaps with acceptance if score improves (or anneal). To speed, maintain counts `cnt` as float/int. Choosing a swap q,p:
- Remove p in S: update cnt by subtracting contributions. Need list S_indices. Use numpy vector update:
```
def apply_flip(cnt, S_bool, S_idx, p, add):
    # update for index p with new value val (1 add, 0 remove)
    if add:
       # old 0 ->1
       # contribution to sums p+q for q in S_idx (these are old S, p not in S)
       idx = S_idx
       sums = p + idx
       valid = sums < cnt.size
       cnt[sums[valid]] += 2
       if 2*p < cnt.size: cnt[2*p] += 1
       S_bool[p]=1; append idx
    else:
       # remove p: first remove self and pairs with current S including p
       # subtract self at 2p
       if 2*p < cnt: cnt[2*p] -=1
       # subtract pairs p+q for q in S_idx where q!=p
       idx = S_idx
       sums=p+idx
       valid=(sums<cnt.size) & (idx!=p)
       cnt[sums[valid]] -=2
       S_bool[p]=0; remove idx
```
- For swap, remove p, compute Rmax, then add q, compute Rmax, accept if improved.
But computing max after each half max scan O(2n). For many swaps, too slow. We can approximate accept based on active coefficient before/after? We can compute maximum of only affected sums by tracking? Need global Rmax. Could maintain a heap of counts? Counts updated O(m) entries; global max can be recomputed when affected entries maybe. Use a dictionary? Numpy max scan 2048 length is cheap. If 100k swaps, 200M C ops maybe okay? 100k*2048=204M, maybe 0.5-1s in C? plus overhead. Good.
- We can restrict candidates: choose p from top violators, q from low regions.
Simplify: Random p in S, q not in S; if Rmax_new < Rmax_old accept; else if equal, accept if score? score with same m only Rmax. Use random to avoid local. This is like hill climbing with flat moves. def greedy_sparse(n, target_score=1.45, min_frac=0.1, batch=32, time_limit=...):
   S = ones(n) bool; m=n; cnt=convolve(S)
   while m>min_frac*n and time:
      T = target_score * m*m / (2*n)
      if cnt.max() <= T: break
      d = np.maximum(cnt - T, 0)
      g = conv_grad(d, S.astype(float))
      V = 2*g
      # subtract self d[2i]
      valid2 = 2*np.arange(n) < len(d)
      V[valid2] -= d[2*arange[valid2]]
      V[S.astype(bool)==0] = -inf
      # choose top batch indices, remove them one by one updating cnt with O(m)
      for p in chosen:
         remove p from S/cnt
         c = cnt (update)
      # periodically recompute cnt? We can update exactly; no need recompute except initial.
```

Let's derive `conv_grad` correctly one more time. For weights p[k] on convolution coefficient k = ∑_j a_j a_{k-j}. Derivative wrt a_i: ∂/∂a_i ∑_j p[k] a_j a_{k-j}. Term j=i gives p[i+k-i? k? Let's compute c_k as sum over u, not treating symmetric. If u=i, derivative a_{k-i}. If also i = k-u, then that same u? The sum over u includes only one term when 2i=k; derivative of a_i^2 =2a_i. The two conditions both correspond to same u=i, so need double? Our earlier derivative 2 a_{k-i} (if valid) includes both u=i and u=k-i, but when i=k-i they coincide and double counts correctly. So derivative = 2 a_{k-i} if valid? For i ≠ k-i, there are two u values: u=i term a_i a_{k-i} derivative a_{k-i}; u=k-i term a_{k-i} a_i derivative a_i = a_{k-i} (since a_i=a_i? Wait a_{k-i} same? If u=k-i, term a_{k-i} a_i derivative wrt a_i is a_{k-i}. Sum 2a_{k-i}. But what about a_i? The term derivative should be a_i? No, in term a_{k-i} a_i, derivative is a_{k-i}; yes. `conv_fft` for a real nonnegative:
```
def conv_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    size=1
    while size < 2*n-1: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    f=fa*fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(f,size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
Negative tiny due FFT; take max(0)? For score, max unaffected by tiny negative. For updates, c should be nonnegative; clip to 0 maybe. `c = np.maximum(c,0)`.

Score normalized = `n * c.max()` (since sum=1 official 2nM; comparing same factor 2). We'll use `score_internal = n*M`. State score maybe official? Use `score_fast` = nM; official =2*score_fast for normalized. But if sequence not normalized official same. We can store official-like `2*score_fast` to compare target. For simplicity state score = official value (approx) = 2*n*M. In local, use internal = n*M for line search.

`update_global(state, seq, score_official)`: normalize seq, compute exact official approx `float(2*len(seq)*conv_fft(seq).max())`, update if better. This recomputes convolution; okay. We can also pass precomputed.

Let's design `optimize_local` in more concrete code:
```
def optimize_local(a, time_limit, rng, state):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n=a.size
    if n<2 or time_limit<=0: return
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_limit
    c=conv_fft(a)
    best_local=2*n*c.max()
    best_seq=a.copy()
    update_global(state,best_seq,best_local)
    it=0
    no_imp=0
    temp_frac=0.25
    momentum=np.zeros(n)
    beta=0.7
    step_sizes=(0.02,0.06,0.15,0.35)
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        it+=1
        M=c.max()
        cur=2*n*M
        if cur < best_local - 1e-11:
            best_local=cur; best_seq=a.copy(); update_global(...); no_imp=0
            temp_frac=max(0.03,temp_frac*0.95)
        else:
            no_imp+=1
        if no_imp > 50:
            a=random_perturb(a,rng,0.18)
            # maybe support flicker
            c=conv_fft(a); temp_frac=min(0.6,temp_frac*1.3); momentum*=0.5; continue
        # active weights
        tol = 0.25*M
        p=np.zeros(c.shape)
        active = c >= max(0, M - temp_frac*M)
        if not np.any(active): active[np.argmax(c)]=True
        # softmax with only active
        x=c[active]/max(temp_frac*M,1e-12)
        x -= x.max(); w=np.exp(x); w/=w.sum(); p[active]=w
        g=2*conv_grad(p,a)
        g -= g.mean()
        momentum = beta*momentum + (1-beta)*g
        scale = norm max momentum
        if scale<1e-14: random perturb
        found=False
        # maybe use current c/M for best candidate
        best_cand=a; best_cand_score=cur
        for s in step_sizes:
            # multiplicative
            lam = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-18))
            logh = lam - s*(scale*momentum)
            logh -= logh.max()
            h=np.exp(logh); h/=h.sum()
            ch=conv_fft(h); sh=n*ch.max() # internal
            if sh < best_cand_score/2 - 1e-12: best...
            # subtractive
            h=a - s*(scale*momentum)
            h=np.clip(h,0,None); ssum=h.sum()
            if ssum>0: h/=ssum
            ch=conv_fft(h); sh=n*ch.max()
            if ...
        if found:
            a=best_cand; c=conv_fft(a); no_imp=0
            # reduce step? no
        else:
            # tiny multiplicative or random
            lam=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-18)); logh=lam - 0.01*(scale*momentum); ... if better accept else random perturb
    final update
```
Need avoid too many conv evaluations when no improvement. In worst, 4 candidates per iteration, each conv. For n=2048, 4* maybe 0.2ms? Actually FFT 4096 complex ~0.1ms? plus Python; 100k iterations -> 400M? Might be too slow. We can reduce candidates to 2 (multiplicative and subtractive) and use fewer iterations. The local optimizer will be called with time budget, not fixed iters. We can adapt: if n > 2048, step_sizes (0.05,0.2) and evaluate only one multiplicative first, if fail subtractive, if fail small random. Multiplicative line search:
```
def apply_multiplicative(a, momentum, scale, s):
   logh = np.log(a+1e-18) - s*scale*momentum
   logh -= logh.max(); h=np.exp(logh); h/=h.sum(); return h
```
Subtractive:
```
h=a - s*scale*momentum; clip; normalize.
```
If a has zeros, log zero = -18; multiplicative may not increase from exact zero. To allow zeros to become positive, occasional random perturb adds. Or use `logh = np.log(a+eps) - ...`; zero becomes small.

We can also try **inverse multiplicative divide by gradient**: `h = a / (1 + s*scale*momentum)` with gradient positive, similar.

Centering momentum: subtract mean. But for multiplicative, centering not necessary because normalization; it helps avoid global scale. Good.

def random_perturb(a,rng,sigma=0.1):
   h=a.copy()
   # mix of global multiplicative and block
   if rng.random()<0.5:
       h *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,a.size))
   else:
       # choose w blocks: 1-5 random intervals multiply by exp(normal)
   # maybe zero flicker:
       k=int(0.05*a.size); idx=rng.choice(a.size,k,replace=False); h[idx]*= rng.choice([0.01,0.1,10,100],k)
   return normalize_nonneg(h)
```

def greedy_support(n, start_frac=0.7, target_score=1.45, min_frac=0.15, batch=16, time_limit=30, rng=None):
    m = int(n*start_frac)
    if start_frac >=1: S = np.ones(n,bool)
    else: idx=rng.choice(n,m,replace=False); S=np.zeros(n,bool); S[idx]=1
    cnt=conv_fft(S.astype(float))  # counts
    m_float=float(m)
    deadline...
    while time and m> n*min_frac:
       target = target_score * m*m / (2*n) # official score target relation: score=2nRmax/m^2 -> Rmax=target_score*m^2/(2n)
       Rmax=cnt.max()
       if Rmax <= target*1.01: break
       d=np.maximum(cnt - target, 0)
       g=conv_grad(d, S.astype(float))
       V=2*g
       idx2=2*np.arange(n)
       valid=idx2<cnt.size
       V[valid]-=d[idx2[valid]]
       V[~S]= -1e18
       # if max V <=0: break
       if V.max() <= 0: break
       # choose top batch indices by V
       top=np.argpartition(V, -min(batch, m))[-batch:]
       for p in top reversed? Need update S/cnt.
           # remove p
           # cnt update exact
           if S[p]:
              # self
              if 2*p < cnt.size: cnt[2*p] -=1
              # cross with current S (including p? remove p from S_idx first? We'll use current S_bool before setting false, excluding p)
              S_idx = np.nonzero(S)[1]  # costly each removal; maintain list?
```

Custom coordinate hill-climb maybe easier to control time and no SciPy quirks:
```
def piecewise_local_search(params, n, K, rng, time_limit):
   best=objective; best_params
   temp=1.0
   while time:
      trial=best_params.copy()
      # perturb 1-2 random dims with normal(0, temp)
      j=rng.integers(0,2K)
      trial[j]+=rng.normal(0,temp)
      if rng.random()<0.2: jump all temp*0.5
      sc=objective(trial)
      if sc<best: accept, temp*=0.95
      else: if rng.random()<exp(-(sc-best)/temp): accept, temp*=0.9?
      temp=max(0.1,min(3,temp*1.02 on reject?))
   return best_seq
```

For local optimizer, starting from best at 512 and upsampling to 2048 should preserve shape. We should run local on 2048/4096 for enough time.

```
def greedy_support(n, start_frac, target_score, min_frac, batch, time_limit, rng):
    if start_frac >= 0.999:
       S = np.ones(n, bool)
    else:
       S = np.zeros(n, bool)
       m0 = int(round(n*start_frac))
       idx = rng.choice(n, m0, replace=False)
       S[idx]=True
    S_idx = np.flatnonzero(S).astype(int)
    m = int(S_idx.size)
    cnt = conv_fft(S.astype(float))
    best_S = S.copy(); best_score = 2*n*cnt.max()/max(1,m*m)
    deadline=...
    while time < deadline and m > max(1, int(min_frac*n)):
       target = target_score * m * m / (2.0*n)
       Rmax = cnt.max()
       cur_score = 2*n*Rmax/(m*m)
       if cur_score < best_score: update best
       if Rmax <= target * 1.02:
           break
       d = np.maximum(cnt - target, 0.0)
       if d.max() <= 0: break
       g = conv_grad(d, S.astype(float))
       V = 2*g
       idx2 = 2*np.arange(n)
       valid = idx2 < cnt.size
       V[valid] -= d[idx2[valid]]
       V[~S] = -1e18
       if V.max() <= 0:
          # random removal
          p = int(S_idx[int(rng.integers(m))])
       else:
          k = min(batch, m)
          top = np.argpartition(V, -k)[-k:]
          p = int(top[-1])  # largest
       # remove p if true
       if not S[p]: ...
       # update cnt: remove self and cross with current S_idx excluding p
       if 2*p < cnt.size: cnt[2*p] -= 1.0
       mask = S_idx != p
       idx_wo = S_idx[mask]
       sums = p + idx_wo
       valid_s = sums < cnt.size
       cnt[sums[valid_s]] -= 2.0
       # due floating, maybe negative small; not clip now
       S[p]=False
       S_idx = S_idx[mask]
       m -= 1
       # occasionally renormalize cnt with FFT to avoid drift
       if (m % 128 == 0) or time...:
           cnt = conv_fft(S.astype(float))
    final best update
    return best_S
```

`optimize_local` important. Let's write it carefully.

```
def optimize_local(a, time_limit, rng, state):
    a = normalize_nonneg(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1))
    n = a.size
    if n < 2 or time_limit <= 1.0:
         add_sequence(state,a); return
    deadline = time.monotonic() + time_limit
    c = conv_fft(a)
    cur = 2.0*n*c.max()
    best_local = cur
    best_seq = a.copy()
    add_sequence(state,best_seq)
    it = 0
    no_imp = 0
    temp_frac = 0.25
    momentum = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    beta = 0.75
    step = 0.12
    global_scale = 1.0
    small_n = n <= 1024
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        M = c.max()
        cur = 2.0*n*M
        if cur < best_local - 1e-11:
            best_local = cur; best_seq=a.copy(); add_sequence(state,best_seq); no_imp=0
            temp_frac = max(0.03, temp_frac*0.94)
            step = max(0.02, step*0.97)
        else:
            no_imp += 1
        if no_imp > (30 if small_n else 50):
            a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.16)
            c = conv_fft(a)
            temp_frac = min(0.6, temp_frac*1.3)
            step = min(0.4, step*1.3)
            momentum *= 0.5
            continue
        # active weights
        tol = max(1e-300, temp_frac * M)
        p = np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=np.float64)
        active = c >= M - tol
        if not np.any(active):
            active[int(np.argmax(c))] = True
        # Also include top few peaks if very few active
        if active.sum() < 3:
             k = max(3, min(c.size, 5))
             top = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
             active[top]=True
        x = c[active] / tol
        x = x - np.max(x)
        w = np.exp(np.clip(x, -30, 0))
        sw = w.sum()
        if sw <= 0 or not np.isfinite(sw):
             w = np.ones_like(c[active]); sw=w.sum()
        p[active] = w / sw
        g = 2.0 * conv_grad(p, a)
        g -= g.mean()
        momentum = beta * momentum + (1.0 - beta) * g
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(momentum)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-15:
             a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.08)
             c = conv_fft(a)
             continue
        # Try multiplicative line search
        accepted = False
        s = step
        for _ in range(3):
             logh = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-18)) - s * scale * momentum
             logh -= np.max(logh)
             h = np.exp(logh)
             hs = h.sum()
             if hs > 0 and np.isfinite(hs):
                 h = h / hs
                 ch = conv_fft(h)
                 sh = 2.0*n*ch.max()
                 if sh < cur - 1e-12:
                      a = h; c = ch; step = min(0.35, s*1.1); accepted=True; no_imp=0; break
             s *= 0.5
        if not accepted:
             # subtractive
             s = step*0.5
             for _ in range(2):
                  h = a - s * scale * momentum
                  h = np.clip(h, 0.0, None)
                  hs = h.sum()
                  if hs > 1e-15:
                      h = h / hs
                      ch = conv_fft(h)
                      sh = 2.0*n*ch.max()
                      if sh < cur - 1e-12:
                          a=h; c=ch; accepted=True; no_imp=0; break
                  s *=0.5
        if not accepted:
             # small random multiplicative
             h = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.03, n))
             hh = h.sum(); if hh>0: h/=hh; ch=conv_fft(h); sh=2*n*ch.max(); if sh<cur: a=h;c=ch; else a=random_perturb(a,rng,0.05); c=conv_fft(a)
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5: break
    # final
    c=conv_fft(a); add_sequence(state,a.copy())
```
This can be slow if `conv_fft` called many. But time managed. We can restrict line search attempts based on n: for n>2048, only 1 multiplicative attempt? Use if small_n else 2. Add:
```
mult_attempts = 3 if n<=1024 else 2
sub_attempts = 2 if n<=1024 else 1
```
Also `random_perturb` each fail may call conv. okay.

Potential bug: `np.exp(logh)` can under/overflow if `s*scale*momentum` large. We clip logh? Use:
```
logh = np.clip(logh, -30, 30) before exp? But normalization relative; we subtract max first. If max huge? scale*momentum maybe O(1) to O(10). s up to0.35, okay. But to be safe, subtract max and clip lower -50.
```
```
logh -= logh.max()
logh = np.clip(logh, -50, 0)
```

`random_perturb` implementation:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.1):
    a = np.asarray(a,float)
    h = a.copy()
    n=h.size
    mode = int(rng.integers(0,4))
    if mode==0:
       h *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
    elif mode==1:
       for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1,4))):
           width = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//4)+1))
           start = int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
           h[start:start+width] *= np.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.2*sigma)))
    elif mode==2:
       k = int(0.05*n)
       idx = rng.choice(n, size=k, replace=False)
       factors = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.5*sigma, k))
       h[idx]*=factors
    else:
       h = a * rng.random(n)
       if h.sum()<=0: h=a.copy()
    return normalize_nonneg(np.maximum(h,0.0))
```
In mode 3, `a*rng.random` may zero many; good.

        state["score"]=float(sc); state["seq"]=a.copy(); print

def score_unormalized(a):

`add_sequence` uses `score_unormalized` (no need normalize first? It clips negatives, scales by sum^2). It also normalizes for storage.
```
def score_unormalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1)
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    c=conv_fft(a)
    return float(2*a.size*c.max()/(s*s))
```
This is scale invariant and matches evaluate except cap? For values <=1000 yes. For our normalized values fine.
In local, `cur=2*n*c.max()` since normalized.

Piecewise objective:
```
def piecewise_objective(params, n, K):
    log_len = params[:K]
    log_h = params[K:2*K]
    z = log_len - np.max(log_len); lengths = np.exp(z); lengths *= (float(n)/lengths.sum())
    base = np.floor(lengths).astype(int)
    rem = int(n - base.sum())
    frac = lengths - base
    order = np.argsort(-frac)
    for idx in order[:rem]: base[idx]+=1
    # if rem negative? base sum might >n due float? floor ≤ lengths sum n, so rem≥0. If due rounding, adjust.
    if base.sum() != n:
       # adjust largest
       while base.sum() < n: base[int(np.argmax(base))]+=1
       while base.sum() > n:
           j=int(np.argmax(base)); 
           if j>=0 and base[j]>0: base[j]-=1
    hnorm = np.exp(np.clip(log_h - np.max(log_h), -30, 30))
    seq = np.repeat(hnorm, base)
    seq = seq[:n]
    # if seq size less due base? if base sum n, ok
    if seq.size != n:
       # fallback
       seq = np.resize(seq, n)
    return score_unormalized(seq)
```
`np.resize` if needed. `np.repeat` with total n may produce n if base sum n.
`log_h` could all equal; fine.

`run_piecewise_search`: We'll use scipy_minimize if available. But Powell with objective that rounds lengths may have poor convergence. We can also do custom coordinate. Let's implement a simple **simulated annealing coordinate** with time allocation, no SciPy needed. However, SciPy maybe better. We'll use both: For each restart, if scipy exists, run Powell with maxiter 30; then take result and coordinate polish.
But `scipy_minimize` requires objective signature `fun(x, *args)`. Our `piecewise_objective(params, n, K)` works.
```
def run_piecewise_search(n,K,rng,budget,state):
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
    dim=2*K
    best_seq=None; best_sc=inf
    restarts = max(1, min(20, int(budget)))? No, each restart.
    while time < deadline:
       init=random piecewise params
       # coordinate warm: 20 iterations
       params=initial; sc=objective
       for _ in range(20):
          trial=params.copy(); j=rng.integers(dim); trial[j]+=rng.normal(0,0.3); sc2=objective
          if sc2<sc: params,sc=trial; else if rng.random()<0.1: accept
       # scipy polish
       if scipy_minimize is not None and remaining>1:
          res=scipy_minimize(piecewise_objective, params, args=(n,K), method='Powell', options={'maxiter':30, 'ftol':1e-8, 'xtol':1e-6})
          if res.fun < sc: params=res.x
       seq = piecewise_seq(params,n,K); scseq=score_unormalized(seq)
       update best; add_sequence(state,seq)
```
Need `random_piecewise_params(K, rng)`:
```
log_len=rng.normal(0,0.5,K)
mode = rng.integers(0,4)
if mode==0: log_h=rng.uniform(-1,1,K)
elif mode==1: log_h=np.linspace(1,-1,K)+rng.normal(0,0.2,K)
elif mode==2: log_h=np.linspace(-1,1,K)+...
else: log_h=np.concatenate([rng.uniform(0,1,K//2), rng.uniform(-1,0,K-K//2), ...? high ends])
```
For K maybe 3: high ends low middle: log_h = [1, -0.5, 1]. But symmetric bad? Explore.

But piecewise full with high ends may be symmetric and central high; still.

Coordinate polish function to avoid too many function evals:
```
def piecewise_polish(params, n, K, rng, time_limit):
   dim=2*K; sc=objective; best=params
   while time:
      trial=best.copy(); step=0.2 + 0.5*rng.random()
      for j in rng.permutation(dim)[:max(1,dim//3)]:
          trial[j]+=rng.normal(0,step)
      sc2=objective
      if sc2<sc: accept step*=0.9
      else: step*=0.95 maybe
   return best,sc
```
Maybe overkill. We can rely on SciPy.

Time for piecewise: We can call for n=256 K=3,4,5 with budget 30s each, total 90. If SciPy not available, custom.

Now, tasks in `propose_candidate`. Let's design high-level flow with dynamic time.
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    rng=...
    hard_deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s - 90
    state=...
    add fallback [1/3,2/3]? Wait score for [1/3,2/3] n=2: conv [1/9,2/9,4/9], max4/9, score=4*(4/9)/(1)=16/9=1.777. uniform n=2 score2. Add best baseline maybe n=3? Try some small exact sequences? We can search small n? For n=2 min? Let's not.
    provided=load; add
    # quick small local from provided/best at n=256/512
    # Instead of tasks, do adaptive:
    if provided: seq=provided
    else: seq=make_initial(256,3,rng)
    # local optimize at 256 for 20s to get state
    optimize_local(seq, 20, rng, state)
    # piecewise search for better starts
    for n,K,budget in [(256,3,30),(256,4,30),(512,3,30),(512,4,45)]:
       if time < deadline: run_piecewise_search(n,K,rng,budget,state)
    # local optimize from several starts at n=512
    for kind in [3,7,8,4,5,9,-1,-2]:
       if time < deadline-100:
          seq=make_initial(512, kind, rng, state["seq"], provided)
          seq=resize_to_length(seq,512)
          optimize_local(seq, 40, rng, state)
    # greedy support
    for n,frac,target in [(1024,1.0,1.55),(1024,0.8,1.45),(2048,0.7,1.45)]:
       if time < deadline-200:
          S=greedy_support(n, frac, target, 0.15, batch=32, time_limit=60, rng=rng)
          if S is not None:
             add_sequence(state, S.astype(float))
             a = normalize_nonneg(S.astype(float))
             if time < deadline-100:
                a = resize_to_length(a, max(1024,a.size))? a already n
                optimize_local(a, 60, rng, state)
    # progressive upsample/local from best to 4096
    cur_n = state["seq"].size if state seq else 512
    max_n = 4096
    while time < deadline-20:
       if cur_n < max_n:
          next_n = min(max_n, max(1024, 2*cur_n))
       else:
          next_n = cur_n
       seq = resize_to_length(state["seq"], next_n)
       rem = hard_deadline - time.monotonic()
       limit = min(120, rem*0.5)
       if limit < 15: break
       old=state["score"]
       optimize_local(seq, limit, rng, state)
       if next_n == cur_n and state["score"] >= old - 1e-9:
          break
       cur_n = state["seq"].size if state["seq"].size>=next_n else next_n
       if next_n == max_n and state["score"] >= old - 1e-9: break
    # final evaluate
    return [float(x) for x in state["seq"]]
```

    best_score=2n cntmax/(m*m); best=S.copy()

       # choose a batch of candidate zeros: random + best heuristic
       # heuristic: compute low fill weights dlow = np.maximum(target - cnt,0)
       dlow = np.maximum(target - cnt, 0)

       # candidate score heuristic h[q] maybe gadd[q] - high_cnt? We want high fill low sums but not exceed target.

```
while time < deadline and m > min_m:
   target = target_score*m*m/(2n)
   Rmax=cnt.max(); cur=2nRmax/m^2; update best
   if Rmax <= target*1.01: break
   d=np.maximum(cnt - target,0)
   if d.max()<=0: break
   g=conv_grad(d, S.astype(float))
   V=2*g - d[2i valid]
   V[~S]=-inf
   if V.max() <= 0: break
   k=min(batch,m)
   chosen = np.unique(np.argpartition(V, -k)[-k:])
   for p in chosen:
       if S[p]:
          old_idx=S_idx
          # remove

          idx = old_idx[old_idx != p]
          sums=p+idx
          valid=sums<cnt.size
          cnt[sums[valid]] -=2
          S[p]=False; S_idx=idx; m-=1
   # after batch, clip cnt small negative? cnt=np.maximum(cnt,0)
   # recompute cnt exact every batch to avoid drift
   cnt = conv_fft(S.astype(float)); cnt = np.maximum(cnt,0)

def greedy_add(S, n, target_score, time_limit, rng, batch=32):
   S=S.astype(bool).copy(); S_idx=np.flatnonzero(S); m=S_idx.size
   if m==0 or m==n: return S
   cnt=conv_fft(S.astype(float))
   best_score=2n*cnt.max()/m*m; best=S.copy(); best_m=m; best_cnt=cnt.copy()
   deadline...
   while time < deadline:
       target = target_score*best_m*best_m/(2n)? For heuristic use current target based on m or best_m?
       target = target_score*m*m/(2n)
       dlow = np.maximum(target - cnt, 0)
       if dlow.max() <= 0 and cnt.max() <= target:
           # no low; try random if cur_score > best?
       dhigh = np.maximum(cnt - target*0.9, 0)
       Sfloat=S.astype(float)
       gadd=conv_grad(dlow,Sfloat)
       ghigh=conv_grad(dhigh,Sfloat)
       score_cand = gadd - 2*ghigh
       score_cand[S]= -1e18
       if score_cand.max() < 0: # maybe random
           candidates = np.flatnonzero(~S)
           chosen = candidates[rng.integers(0,candidates.size,batch)]
       else:
           k=min(batch, n-m)
           chosen=np.unique(np.argpartition(score_cand, -k)[-k:])
       for q in chosen:
           if q<n and not S[q]:
              old_idx=S_idx; old_m=m
              sums=q+old_idx; valid=sums<cnt.size
              # apply
              cnt[sums[valid]] += 2
              if 2*q<cnt.size: cnt[2*q] +=1
              S[q]=True; S_idx=np.append(old_idx,q); m+=1
              cur=2n*cnt.max()/(m*m)

              else:
                  # revert
                  cnt[sums[valid]] -=2
                  if 2*q<cnt.size: cnt[2*q]-=1
                  S[q]=False; S_idx=old_idx; m=old_m

   return best

Let's think of exact finite construction from **square root of a constant polynomial**. We want P(x)^2 coefficients ≤0.75. If P has nonnegative coefficients sum1. Could use **uncorrelated blocks**: Divide sequence into blocks of length B. Treat block masses. Convolution at fine level = sum over block pair products times intra-block convolutions. If intra-block sequences are orthogonal/flat, the total max may be controlled. This is a recursive construction! We can construct sequences recursively to approach lower bound. For example, let base sequence b of length L with low convolution. Then form a as sum of shifted copies of b with weights w at block positions: a = ∑_i w_i b_{x-iL}. Convolution a*a = ∑_{i,j} w_i w_j (b*b shifted by (i+j)L). If b*b scaled is flat relative to its mass, the max coefficient is ∑ w_i w_j overlap of shifted b*b. If b*b support length <L and shifts by multiples L, the summands for different block pair sums (i+j) are separated in time and don't overlap! Then max = max over s ∑_{i+j=s} w_i w_j * c_b_max. This is exactly the same objective at block scale! Thus if b has score S_b (max_block * (L?)), the composite score maybe S_block? Let's derive.
Let b length L sum1. Let a on length N=B*L with blocks of length L, weight w_i placed in block i (same b scaled w_i). Then sum a = ∑w_i. Convolution coefficient at time (sL + t) = (∑_{i+j=s} w_i w_j) * (b*b)_t. Max = max_s A_s * max_b. Score =2 (BL) max_s A_s max_b / (∑w)^2. If b normalized sum1, max_b. The block objective score for weights w is 2B max A_s / (∑w)^2. Composite score = L * [score_block] * [L? Wait max_b * BL = B L max_b max A. score_block=2B maxA/W^2. score_b=2L max_b. Composite = (L/2?) Let's compute: composite =2BL maxA max_b/W^2 = (L * 2B maxA/W^2) * (2L max_b)/(2L?) No. = L * (2B maxA/W^2) * max_b. score_b=2L max_b, so composite = (score_block * score_b)/2? Since score_block=2B maxA/W^2, composite = L * score_block * max_b = (score_b/2)*score_block. Interesting. If both scores <2, product/2 can be lower. For uniform b score_b=2, composite=score_block. If we have a base b with score_b maybe 2? no improvement. If we have recursive with scores below2, product reduces? If target 1.5, base 1.5 and block 1.5 composite =1.125? But recursive may allow? However b*b support length 2L-1, shifts by L cause overlap if L <2L (always): block pair sums s and s+1 support overlap in last L? Since b*b length 2L-1, shifts by L overlap. To avoid overlap, need block size L > 2L-1 impossible. Could use empty gap of length G between blocks so a length B*(L+G) with b length L and gap G. Then shifts of b*b by (L+G); support length 2L-1. If gap G+? For nonoverlap between shifts differing by block stride D=L+G, need 2L-1 ≤ D => G≥L-1. Then N≈B*2L, large, zeros. Then composite score? Non-overlap max = max_s A_s * max_b. Sum a=∑w, length N=B D. Composite =2 B D maxA max_b/W^2. Block objective on stride B: score_block=2B maxA/W^2. composite = D * score_block * max_b = D*(score_b/2)*score_block (since score_b=2L max_b). D/L ≈2, composite ≈ score_block * score_b. If both 1.5, composite 2.25 worse. So recursion not trivial.

But if b has small support length relative to stride? This is like hierarchical supports; may not beat direct.

Another construction: Use **independent random weights in blocks** and average over many blocks to reduce peaks? Let a_i = sqrt? If choose block weights w_i random with zero-mean fluctuations, cross terms average out. Composite = (constant component)^2? The DC part gives score2; deviations can cancel central. This is similar to Fourier flat.

Maybe use **randomized rounding of a continuous flat design**. Hard.

`piecewise_seq(params,n,K)`: top-level.
```
def piecewise_seq(params,n,K):
   log_len=params[:K]
   log_h=params[K:2*K]
   zl=log_len - max
   lengths=n*np.exp(zl)/sum
   base=floor(lengths).astype(int)
   rem=n-base.sum()
   if rem>0:
      frac=lengths-base
      order=np.argsort(-frac)
      for idx in order[:rem]: base[idx]+=1
   elif rem<0:
      # decrement largest until sum n
      order=np.argsort(base)
      idx=0
      while rem<0:
          l=base[idx]
          if l>1:
             base[idx]-= min(l-1, -rem); rem+= min(l-1,-rem)
          idx=(idx+1)%K
   h=np.exp(np.clip(log_h - max, -30, 30))
   seq=np.repeat(h, base)
   if seq.size != n: seq=np.resize(seq,n)
   return normalize_nonneg(seq)
```
Potential `rem` can be large if floor sum much less due n small? floor sum ≤ n- K, rem≥K. Loop over order[:rem] if rem large but rem<n; order length K may be less than rem if K<rem! Example n=256,K=3, floor sum could 253, rem3, okay ≤K? Since sum lengths=n, each fractional part <1, floor sum = n - sum frac, rem = sum frac < K. So rem < K. Good. If n<K, floor sum maybe 0, rem=n <K? order length K, okay. Decrement rem negative not happen.
`name = np.max(log_len)` if K=0 no.

`build_active_matrix(f, rows)`: top-level.
```
def build_active_matrix(f, rows):
    n=f.size; rows=np.asarray(rows,int)
    A=np.zeros((rows.size,n),float)
    for r,k in enumerate(rows):
       jlo=max(0, int(k)-n+1); jhi=min(n, int(k)+1)
       if jhi>jlo:
          A[r,jlo:jhi] = f[int(k)-np.arange(jlo,jhi)]
    return A
```

def lp_direction(a,c,active_weights,max_rows=100):
    if _scipy_linprog is None or a.size>1536: return None
    n=a.size; m=c.size
    if max_rows >= m: rows=np.arange(m)
    else: rows=np.argpartition(c,-max_rows)[-max_rows:]
    A=build_active_matrix(a,rows)
    if not np.any(A): return None
    g=2*conv_grad(active_weights,a); g-=g.mean()
    if np.allclose(g,0): return None
    # center g? sum delta=0 makes constant irrelevant; center good
    low=-a
    high=0.5*np.max(a)+1.0/n
    # ensure high > low; if a_i 0 high positive
    Aeq=np.ones((1,n))
    try:
       res=_scipy_linprog(g, A_ub=2*A, b_ub=c[rows]*0? wait constraint c_k + 2 A_k δ <= M + margin. Use slack = M*1.05 - c[rows]
       M = float(c.max()); slack = M*1.05 - c[rows]
       # If slack negative for rows >M? no rows near M. Use np.maximum(slack, 1e-12)
       slack=np.maximum(slack,1e-12)
       bounds=list(zip(low, high))
       res=_scipy_linprog(g, A_ub=2*A, b_ub=slack, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=np.zeros(1), bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except: return None
    if res is not None and res.status==0 and res.x is not None:
       delta=np.asarray(res.x,float)
       delta=np.clip(delta, low, high)
       if np.max(np.abs(delta))>1e-15: return delta
    return None
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    hard_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 80.0)
    state = {"score": float("inf"), "seq": None}
    add_sequence(state, [0.3333333333333333, 0.6666666666666666])
    add_sequence(state, np.ones(16))
    provided = load_provided_sequence()
    if provided is not None: add_sequence(state, provided)
    # quick local on provided/best length 256/512
    if state["seq"] is not None and 64 <= state["seq"].size <= 2048:
        seq = resize_to_length(state["seq"], min(512, max(256, state["seq"].size)))
        limit = min(30.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic())
        if limit > 5: optimize_local(seq, limit, rng, state)
    # piecewise
    pw_budgets = [(256,3,30),(256,4,35),(512,3,45),(512,4,50),(512,5,50)]
    for n,K,b in pw_budgets:
       if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 200:
          run_piecewise_search(n,K,rng,min(b, hard_deadline-now-200), state)
    # local starts at 512
    kinds = [3,7,8,4,5,9,0,1,-1,-2]
    for kind in kinds:
       if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 300:
          seq = make_initial(512, kind, rng, state["seq"], provided)
          limit = min(35.0, hard_deadline - now - 300)
          if limit > 5: optimize_local(seq, limit, rng, state)
    # greedy support
    greedy_tasks = [(1024,1.0,1.55,0.15,60),(1024,0.7,1.45,0.25,70),(2048,0.8,1.5,0.2,80),(2048,0.5,1.4,0.3,80)]
    for n, frac, target, minfrac, b in greedy_tasks:
       if time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 400:
           S = greedy_support(n, frac, target, minfrac, 32, b, rng)
           if S is not None:
              add_sequence(state, S.astype(float))
              a = normalize_nonneg(S.astype(float))
              if time<deadline-250:
                  # maybe upscale local at min(n,2048)
                  limit=min(50, deadline-250)
                  optimize_local(a, limit, rng, state)
    # progressive upsample from best
    if state["seq"] is None: return fallback
    cur_n = state["seq"].size
    max_n = 4096
    same_no_gain=0
    while time < hard_deadline - 30:
        if cur_n < max_n:
            next_n = min(max_n, max(1024, 2*cur_n))
        else: next_n=cur_n
        seq = resize_to_length(state["seq"], next_n)
        rem = hard_deadline - time.monotonic()
        limit = min(120.0, rem*0.6)
        if limit < 10: break
        old=state["score"]
        optimize_local(seq, limit, rng, state)
        if next_n == cur_n and state["score"] >= old - 1e-9:
            same_no_gain += 1
            if same_no_gain >= 2: break
        else:
            same_no_gain=0
        cur_n = state["seq"].size if state["seq"] is not None else next_n
    # final resize best to maybe 2048/4096 if score improves? If state.seq shorter and deadline enough, upsample and quick local 60s
    final = state["seq"]
    if final is not None and time.monotonic() < hard_deadline:
        # check official
        try:
           official = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final])
           if np.isfinite(official): print
        except: pass
    return [float(x) for x in final]
```

Implement `run_piecewise_search` time-aware:
```
def run_piecewise_search(n,K,rng,budget,state):
    if budget <= 2: return
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
    dim=2*K
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        params = random_piecewise_params(K,rng)
        sc = piecewise_objective(params,n,K)
        # coordinate 40 or until deadline
        start=time.monotonic()
        for _ in range(60):
            if time.monotonic()+0.2 > deadline: break
            trial=params.copy()
            step = 0.25 + 0.5*rng.random()
            for _ in range(int(rng.integers(1, max(2, dim//3)+1))):
                j=int(rng.integers(0,dim))
                trial[j] += rng.normal(0, step)
            sc2 = piecewise_objective(trial,n,K)
            if sc2 < sc:
                params=trial; sc=sc2
            else:
                delta=sc2-sc
                if delta < 0.05 and rng.random() < np.exp(-delta/0.08):
                    params=trial; sc=sc2
        if sc < state["score"]: # maybe
        seq=piecewise_seq(params,n,K); update_state(state,seq,sc)
        # if good, maybe scipy polish if budget remains
        if _scipy_minimize is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
            try:
                res=_scipy_minimize(piecewise_objective, params, args=(n,K), method='Powell', options={'maxiter':12,'ftol':1e-8})
                if res.fun < sc:
                    params=res.x; sc=res.fun; seq=piecewise_seq(...); update...
            except: pass
```
Need not overdo.

Now, `conv_grad` implementation details:
```
def conv_grad(p,a):
    n=a.size; m=p.size
    size=1
    need=n+m-1
    while size<need: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],size)
    r=np.fft.irfft(fa*fp,size)
    return r[m-1-np.arange(n)]
```
We need ensure `p[::-1]` length m; zero padding to size. Good. The result length size; we take indices from m-1 down to m-n. m=2n-1, m-1=2n-2, m-n=n-2. For n=2, indices [1,0], as tested? Earlier r[2]=g0, r[1]=g1; m-1=2 => r[2],r[1]. Good. For n=1? not used. Good.
FFT can produce tiny imaginary? irfft real. 

`conv_fft` uses `fa*fa` not `fa*fa.conj()` because sequence real, square complex? FFT of real, square (not abs) corresponds to convolution? For real a, FFT(a)*FFT(a) inverse gives circular convolution (a*a) (not correlation). Yes, no conjugate. For autocorrelation (a*rev) would use abs^2. Good.
Need size >=2n-1 to avoid wrap.

def conv_fft(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    size=1; need=2*n-1
    while size<need: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)
    c=c[:need]
    return np.maximum(c,0.0)
```
